Spinal Cord Injuries

Spine · 54 / 69

1Basics

Epidemiology

Incidence11,000/yr US · incomplete tetraplegia 34% (central cord commonest) · complete paraplegia 25% · complete tetraplegia 22% · incomplete paraplegia 17%

Distributionbimodal: young high-energy · elderly minor trauma + spondylotic stenosis · 50% cervical · MVA 50%, falls, GSW · 3–25% occur after the initial trauma (poor immobilisation/transport)

Pathophysiology

Primary injurydirect, irreversible

Secondary injury↓ perfusion, lipid peroxidation, free radicals/cytokines, apoptosis → target of methylprednisolone

Associatedhead injury, non-contiguous spine fractures, vertebral artery injury (atlas fractures, facet dislocations; usually asymptomatic unilateral; MRA least invasive; stent only if basilar insufficiency symptoms)

3Presentation

Spinal vs neurogenic vs hypovolaemic shock

Spinal shock Neurogenic shock Hypovolaemic
BP ↓ ↓ ↓
Pulse Bradycardia Bradycardia Tachycardia
Bulbocavernosus reflex Absent Variable / can be intact Variable
Motor Flaccid areflexic Variable Variable
Mechanism Neurons hyperpolarised, unresponsive Loss of sympathetic tone (injury above T6), ↓ SVR, pooling, unopposed vagus ↓ preload
Skin / urine — Warm extremities, urine output preserved (✗ Wrong options: absent urine output) Cold

Bulbocavernosus reflexanal contraction on squeezing glans/clitoris or tugging the Foley · its return = end of spinal shock · conus/cauda equina injury → may never return
✗ Wrong options: "returns in all cauda equina injuries" (TO)

Spinal shock ruled outBCR present or 48 h elapsed

Spinal shock phases

Phase Orthobullets TO (Ditunno)
1 0–48 h areflexia 0–24 h areflexia; delayed plantar first, then BCR, abdominal, cremasteric
2 1–2 days: polysynaptic (BCR) return, monosynaptic (patellar) absent Day 1–3: cutaneous reflexes, DTRs absent
3 1–4 wks hyperreflexia Day 4–1 month: DTRs return, Babinski
4 1–12 months spasticity 1–12 months: hyperreflexia, autonomic dysreflexia

First reflex to returndelayed plantar (TO) · classical teaching bulbocavernosus — read the options · order: delayed plantar → BCR → cremasteric → ankle jerk → Babinski → knee jerk

4Diagnosis & Classification

ASIA impairment scale

Grade Motor Sensory
A Complete None None (incl. S4-5)
B Incomplete None (none > 3 levels below NLI) Preserved (incl. sacral)
C Incomplete Preserved; > half key muscles < grade 3 Preserved
D Incomplete Preserved; ≥ half key muscles ≥ grade 3 Preserved
E Normal Normal

Diagnostic criteria – classifying an injury

Step 1 – neurologic levellowest segment with normal motor + sensory both sides (e.g. intact wrist extension, absent triceps → C6)

Step 2 – complete?no voluntary anal contraction + 0/5 distal motor + no perianal sensation + BCR intact → complete

Step 3 – gradeany perianal sensation = at least B (sacral sparing → incomplete)

BCR absent → cannot assign ASIA grade (spinal shock) · prognosis by repeated examinations over 48–72 h
✗ Wrong options: MRI · SSEP · EMG

Descriptivetetraplegia (cervical: arms, trunk, legs, pelvis) · paraplegia (arms spared)

ASIA B at C6 (OB)deltoid 5/5, long-finger + medial forearm sensation may be present, intrinsics 0/5 · no triceps power (2/5 OB; 3/5 in the unkeyed TO variant)

Incomplete cord syndromes

Syndrome Mechanism Deficit Prognosis
Central cord (commonest) Hyperextension in elderly spondylotic neck (cord pinched between body + buckled flavum) UE > LE, distal > proximal (UE fibres medial), sacral sparing Good
Brown-Séquard Hemisection, penetrating Ipsilateral motor + proprioception/vibration, contralateral pain + temp (2–3 levels below) Best
Anterior cord Hyperflexion / compression, anterior spinal artery (e.g. aortic dissection) Motor + pain/temp lost, proprioception preserved Worst (~90% no recovery)
Posterior cord Rare (tabes dorsalis atraumatic mimic) Proprioception lost —
Conus / cauda equina — — Conus recovers better than proximal lesions

Rear-end collision → quadriparesis (TO)extension–distraction (whiplash mechanism)

5Management

Field & initial care

Immobilisationrigid collar + spine board (transport only) · decubiti after 30–60 min on board · log roll · athletes: leave helmet + shoulder pads on until controlled simultaneous removal

Airwayinjury above C5 → likely intubation

Examremove collar, inspect face/scalp, rotational head posture (unilateral facet dislocation), posterior midline tenderness (absent in alert patient → low probability of injury), log roll, neuro exam · seat-belt sign → flexion-distraction

PreventionDVT prophylaxis (LMWH, rotating bed, pneumatic compression) · avoid hypotension · pulmonary toilet · skin

Neurogenic shock treatment

TOIV fluids first → vasopressors/inotropes if persists · phenylephrine (pure α1 → reflex bradycardia) · bradycardia: atropine, glycopyrrolate (before suction), isoproterenol, theophylline/aminophylline

OBhypotension unresponsive to initial fluids → pressors · Swan-Ganz-guided fluids
✗ Wrong options: more rapid fluid → overload, CHF

Methylprednisolone

Currentnot recommended: no clear benefit, ↑ complications (infection, GI bleed, ventilator days, LOS) · no effect on cord oedema · OB variant: "controversial, may be given in appropriate circumstances" — read the options

Historical NASCIS IIInon-penetrating SCI within 8 h · 30 mg/kg bolus + 5.4 mg/kg/h for 23 h (< 3 h) or 47 h (3–8 h) · CI: GSW, pregnancy, < 13 yrs, > 8 h, peripheral nerve injury

Presenting at 10 hdon't start methylprednisolone

GM-1controversial; no long-term benefit · hypothermia — not recommended (coagulopathy, sepsis, pneumonia, arrhythmia)

Reduction & surgery

Closed reductionawake, alert patient with deficit from fracture-dislocation (commonest: bilateral facet dislocation) · add 10 lb every 10 min with exams · abort: overdistraction, neuro worsening (→ remove traction), failure

TO (stable C4-5 fracture-dislocation with weakness)MRI first (cord, compression, disc)
✗ Wrong options: immediate traction

Decompression + stabilisationmost incomplete and complete SCI (except GSW) · early (< 24 h) improves recovery even in complete injury · may recover 1–2 root levels · stabilise for rehab

GSWmost → brace / treat extraspinal injury (stable lateral masses, no CSF leak) · surgery for progressive deficit with canal bullet, cauda equina, bullet in thecal sac (lead poisoning)

C6 SCI + spondylotic C6 fractureearly decompression + stabilisation alone
✗ Wrong options: steroids · hypothermia

Functional level

Level Function
C1-3 Ventilator; electric chair head/chin control
C3-4 Ventilator → may wean; head/chin (C4 puffer)
C5 Deltoid, biceps · electric wheelchair with hand control · can't feed (no wrist extension/supination)
C6 Wrist extension + supination → feeds self · manual wheelchair + sliding-board transfers · drives with hand controls
C7 Triceps · manual wheelchair + independent transfers
C8-T1 Hand dexterity · fully independent transfers
T2-6 Normal UE, wheelchair
T7-12 Abdominal control; bracing walk possible
L1-5 Variable; aids
S1-5 Walk with minimal assistance

Posterior deltoid → triceps transfercomplete C5/C6 with 5/5 deltoid + biceps, 0/5 triceps
✗ Wrong options: axillary nerve injury · weak deltoid

FESstimulates skeletal muscle

TENSpresynaptic inhibition in the dorsal horn (small myelinated afferents block C-fibre input) + endorphins

6Complications

Complication Fact
Pressure injury Prevention: frequent repositioning, off board early · NPUAP 2016: "injury", Arabic stages (TO keys: "Grade II pressure injury" FNB 2022 · "Grade 2 pressure injury" — read the options)
VTE Immediate prophylaxis
Urosepsis Common cause of death
Sinus bradycardia Commonest arrhythmia acutely
Orthostatic / supine hypotension, arrhythmia Lost sympathetic control (T6 or above) · not spasticity (not sympathetic)
Autonomic dysreflexia Above T5-6 · headache, agitation, hypertension (SBP ↑ ≥ 20%), bradycardia, sweating · unchecked visceral stimulus → check Foley, disimpact, X-ray for occult fracture (swollen thigh)
Major depression ~11% (5–20%) · suicidal ideation
Shoulder Long-term paraplegia → 4× rotator cuff tears
Post-traumatic instability Missed adjacent ligamentous slip below construct → revise + extend fusion

Prognosis

Complete80% gain 1 root level, 20% gain 2 · 1% full recovery · ASIA A: least recovery, highest lifetime cost (ASIA B 46.6% marked recovery)

Incompletemore sparing + faster early recovery = better · plateau rarely resumes

MRI predicting complete injuryintramedullary cord haematoma
✗ Wrong options: oedema · ALL disruption · prevertebral oedema

SCIWORAcommoner < 10 yrs · severity of initial deficit = best predictor

Dural tears (TO)

Commonest sitelateral, then root sheath, axilla · suture whenever possible · sealants (Tachosil, fibrin glue) if not · small unsuturable → tight fascia + skin
✗ Wrong options: posterior

SIADH vs cerebral salt wasting (TO)

SIADH Cerebral salt wasting
Volume Euvolaemic / hypervolaemic Hypovolaemic
Serum osm < 275 ↓
Urine osm > 100 —
Urine Na > 40 mEq/L High
Criteria exclude Diuretics, thyroid/adrenal disease (✗ Wrong options: renal insufficiency is not a criterion) —
Supplementary Uric acid < 4, corrected by fluid restriction, ↑ ADH —
Treatment Na < 125 acute/severe → 3% saline + furosemide · correct ≤ 8 mEq/L per 24 h (osmotic demyelination) · chronic → fluid restriction, demeclocycline, conivaptan Saline

Whiplash (TO)

Mechanismrear impact → lower cervical hyperextension + upper flexion (S-shape) · commonest MVA injury (up to 83%) · "WAD"

Poor outcome predictorsrapid-onset pain, severe early pain, hospital admission, radiation to arm, headache · least predictive: pain isolated to neck (TO key)

ManagementMRI not initially (false positives) · early mobilisation > soft collar

How it's asked

"Spinal shock — EXCEPT?"BCR returns in all cauda equina injuries (TO)

"Cervical fracture-dislocation, no motor/sensation/anal tone, BCR intact?"complete SCI (TO)

"Spinal shock — HR, BP, BCR?"bradycardia, hypotension, absent BCR (TO)

"First reflex to return after spinal shock?"delayed plantar (TO)

"ASIA B at C6 — NOT seen?"triceps power (TO unkeyed; OB: 2/5 triceps)

"Thoracic trauma, bradycardia, no catheter-tug response — ASIA?"cannot be determined (TO unkeyed; OB same scenario keyed)

"Wrist extension 5/5, triceps 0/5, no sacral function, BCR intact?"complete C6 (ASIA A)

"Grade 3 in most muscles, sensation intact, BCR intact?"ASIA D

"No motor, diminished perianal + LE sensation?"ASIA B

"ASIA B motor definition?"no motor > 3 levels below NLI

"Grade 2 power in 80% of LE key muscles, sacral sensation intact?"ASIA C

"Complete injury — early decompression?"improves chance of neurologic recovery

"Biceps 4/5 left, deltoid 5/5, nothing below, no sacral function?"complete C5

"No recovery at 48 h with BCR back?"complete cord syndrome

"BCR absent — best prognostic tool?"repeat physical examinations

"Diver, HR 57, BP 92/70, absent BCR?"unable to determine ASIA

"Neurogenic shock — NOT seen?"absent urine output (TO)

"Hypotension + relative bradycardia, BCR present?"neurogenic shock

"Expected vitals in neurogenic shock?"HR 55, BP 65/40

"Hypotension unresponsive to fluids after cervical SCI?"pressors

"Methylprednisolone 10 h after injury?"don't start

"Methylprednisolone in SCI — evidence?"not supported; more complications, no clear benefit

"C6 fracture SCI — treatment?"early decompression + stabilisation

"ASIA A C5-6 facet dislocation — steroids?"controversial, may be given in appropriate circumstances

"Best prognosis syndrome?"Brown-Séquard (TO)

"Central cord mechanism?"hyperextension in elderly with hypertrophied facets + flavum (TO)

"Worst prognosis syndrome?"anterior cord (TO)

"Rear-end crash, quadriparesis?"extension–distraction (TO)

"82-yr hyperextension, UE weakness, LE spared?"central cord syndrome

"Left neck GSW, Brown-Séquard?"left limb weakness, right pain/temp loss

"LE weakness + pain/temp loss after aortic dissection, proprioception kept?"anterior cord syndrome

"Brown-Séquard deficits?"ipsilateral motor, contralateral pain + temp

"Right C7 pain/temp loss, left C5 motor + vibration loss?"left hemisection at C5

"Complete C5 function?"electric wheelchair with hand control

"Deltoid-to-triceps transfer candidate?"C6 ASIA A, 5/5 biceps

"C6 SCI maximal function?"manual wheelchair + sliding-board transfers

"SCIWORA — best predictor?"severity of initial neurologic injury

"MRI predicting complete SCI?"6-mm cord haematoma

"Lowest recovery + highest cost?"ASIA A

"Quadriplegic sacral sore — description?"Grade II / Grade 2 pressure injury (TO — keys differ)

"T6 lesion — NOT sympathetic loss?"spasticity

"FES acts on?"skeletal muscle

"MDD in SCI?"5–20%

"Paraplegic since 14 — shoulder MRI?"rotator cuff tear

"Slip below thoracolumbar construct on sitting films?"revise + extend posterior fusion

"T3 paraplegic, dysreflexia, swollen thigh?"occult fracture

"ASIA C → B during traction?"remove traction

"Low-velocity GSW, stable, no CSF leak?"treat extraspinal injuries + orthosis

"TENS mechanism?"presynaptic inhibition in the dorsal horn

"C4-5 fracture-dislocation with weakness, stable — next?"MRI (TO)

"Not an SIADH criterion?"renal insufficiency (TO)

"SIADH vs CSW?"euvolaemia (TO)

"Dural tears — true?"suture whenever possible (TO)

"Least predictive of poor whiplash outcome?"pain isolated to neck (TO)

Spinal Cord Monitoring

Spine · 55 / 69

1Basics

Modalities

Modality Monitors Stimulate → record Accuracy
SSEP Dorsal columns (afferent; gracilis LE, cuneatus UE) Posterior tibial (ankle) / peroneal; ulnar/median → transcranial recording of somatosensory cortex (or neck) 25% sensitive, 100% specific
tcMEP Lateral + ventral corticospinal tracts (efferent; anterior 2/3, ASA territory) Transcranial motor cortex → muscle (gastroc, soleus, EHL) 100% sensitive, 100% specific · most sensitive early indicator
Spontaneous (mechanical) EMG Specific nerve roots Surgical manipulation → muscle · burst = contact (insignificant) · sustained train = significant traction/injury Over-sensitive
Triggered (electrical) EMG Pedicle breach (medial/inferior wall → exiting root) Stimulate screw → muscle · bone insulates —
Stagnara wake-up test Gross motor (move feet) Intraoperative waking (amnestic) Not continuous; delays
SSEP MEP
Anaesthetic effect Unaffected / reliable Highly affected (TO trap: "MEPs not affected" = false)
Anterior cord ischaemia Unreliable (paralysis with normal SSEP reported) Detects
Existing motor deficit — Still measurable (TO)

4Diagnosis & Classification

Alert criteria

Modality Significant change
SSEP ≥ 50% ↓ amplitude and/or ≥ 10% ↑ latency (or loss) · not 10% amplitude / 50% latency
MEP > 50% ↓ amplitude · > 100 V ↑ threshold (early injury)
Triggered EMG < 8 mA → breach (Orthobullets, OB) · Stecker: < 10 mA inspect, < 5 mA likely malpositioned · > 8 mA (e.g. 11, 12, 14 mA) = intact pedicle

Lumbar (L4-5) surgery (TO)triggered EMG
✗ Wrong options: SSEP · MEP · D-waves (cord monitors)

Patient with existing motor deficit (TO)transcranial MEPs

5Management

Responding to signal loss

Significant signal change
  • Check technical→LEADS, EQUIPMENT, interference (irrigation, cautery, fluoroscopy, warming blanket)
  • Check anaesthesia→NO PARALYTIC / INHALATIONAL bolus, steady state
  • Optimise physiology→RAISE MAP (80–90 TO · > 90 OB), Hb > 8, O2, reverse hypocapnia + hypothermia
  • Surgical→REVERSE LAST MANOEUVRE (distraction/correction), REMOVE IMPLANT, widen decompression
    (signals may take ≥ 10 min to return; close 3-column osteotomy)
  • Still absent→STAGNARA WAKE-UP TEST

TO EXCEPT✗ Wrong options: lower BP to reduce blood loss (raising MAP recovers signals in ~50%)

Corpectomy cage placed → 75% SSEP ↓ + MEP lossremove the cage and monitor

Deformity correction → sustained 80% SSEP ↓, technical + anaesthetic causes excludedreverse correction ± wake-up test
✗ Wrong options: steroids · remove all instrumentation · continue

DMD child, diffuse 80% MEP ↓, MAP 55 on propofolrestore MAP > 90 and retest
✗ Wrong options: remove one screw (diffuse change) · switch to halogenated gas

Most concerning in AIS correction65% drop in LE MEPs
✗ Wrong options: 30% SSEP ↓ · 5% latency ↑ · 14 mA screw

Most concerning in PSO> 100 V MEP threshold rise

Reversible causes

Commonest non-anaesthetic reversible causepatient positioning (nerve compression, plexus traction, neck alignment)
✗ Wrong options: screw · ischaemia · retractor

Unilateral cortical SSEP loss only during ACDF (subcortical + Erb's normal)cerebral ischaemia from carotid retraction (incomplete circle of Willis)

Hypothermia SSEP drop (TO)flood the field with warm saline (fastest — exposed pathway)
✗ Wrong options: warmer air, room, blankets

Physiological effects on evoked potentials (TO)

Variable Effect
Cerebral blood flow Normal to 20 · deteriorate 18–13 · absent < 10–12 mL/100 g/min
Hypotension ↑ latency
↑ ICP ↑ latency, ↓ amplitude
PaO₂ Deteriorate < 40 mmHg
Haematocrit Latency ↑ at 10–15%; amplitude ↓ < 10%
PaCO₂ Extreme hypocapnia < 25 mmHg
Hypothermia ↑ latency; no recording < 18 °C
Inhalational agents Depress: N₂O > isoflurane > halothane
IV agents Propofol no spinal effect (cortical depression recovers fast) · opioids, ketamine none · benzodiazepines mild

EMG myotomes (TO)

Root Muscle
C5 Deltoid, biceps
C6 Biceps, triceps, brachioradialis, PT, FCR
C7 Triceps, PT, FCR, forearm extensors
C8 / T1 Hand intrinsics (APB, FDI, ADM), FCU
L2–L4 Iliopsoas, adductors, quadriceps
L5 Tibialis anterior, peroneus longus
S1-2 Gastrocnemius, abductor hallucis
S2–S5 Anal, urethral sphincter
CN VII / XI / XII Facial muscles / SCM + trapezius / tongue

How it's asked

"Neuromonitoring — EXCEPT?""tcMEP unaffected by anaesthetics vs SSEP" (TO)

"Monitoring in a patient with existing motor deficit?"transcranial MEPs (TO)

"AIS fusion monitoring — true?"SSEPs monitor the dorsal columns

"SSEPs — true?"unreliable for anterior cord pathways

"Best detector of evolving motor tract injury in cervical surgery?"tcMEP

"Most sensitive early indicator of cord injury?"transcranial MEPs

"SSEP alert criteria?"50% ↓ amplitude, 10% ↑ latency
